People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I do flooring, and you spend all day looking down at mistakes—seams, cuts, corners that don’t line up—and I guess my head gets stuck like that too. But… yeah. That’s why I’m here.

Not really… I mean, I used to have things. I’d paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals, like too much food for everybody, and I used to run before jobs because it cleared my head. And theater rehearsals—I used to actually show up for those, even when I was tired.

It’s… it’s a lot of little things that feel like they all point back to me. Like my husband got worse, and I keep thinking if I’d been different—less heavy, less… whatever I am—maybe he wouldn’t have. And with the crew, when we lost work, I kept thinking, that’s because of me too. Like I brought something bad onto the job.

Even clients’ houses… if a floor didn’t come out right, or something got messed up, I’d look at it and think, see, that’s what happens when I touch things. I poison everything. And then my brain says, well, if you ruin people’s homes and your family and the people depending on you, you deserve whatever happens.

Sorry, I know that sounds dramatic. I keep thinking about this one kitchen tile job where the grout color was off, and the homeowner kept staring at it like I’d ruined her whole life. It wasn’t even just the grout, it was like proof. Like everywhere I go, I leave damage.

Yeah… my energy is just gone. Like, I’ll carry one box of tile and it feels like my whole body turns into wet cement. And then I’ll try to answer a couple client messages, just normal stuff, and my head gets so tired I can’t even decide what to say back.

I mean… not like I can separate it cleanly. The not waking up thought is there a lot, and then sometimes it turns into, like, maybe dying would be what I deserve. Like a punishment that would balance out what I’ve done to my family and the crew.

Not really. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.

And theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.

Even if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. So… no, I don’t really enjoy things.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n8\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I… I came in because I keep having these thoughts that maybe it’d be better if I just didn’t wake up. Like, I know that sounds bad, but it keeps coming back. And sometimes I think dying would be… like, the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family, and my crew too. I do flooring, and you spend all day looking down at mistakes—seams, cuts, corners that don’t line up—and I guess my head gets stuck like that too. But… yeah. That’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how long… it feels like it’s been sitting there for a while, like a stain you keep seeing even when the light changes. I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn’t wake up, and then my mind goes off—like if I messed things up for my family or for my crew, maybe dying would be the fair punishment. I know that’s not really answering with a number… it just keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like your work or spending time with your family?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I used to have things. I’d paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals, like too much food for everybody, and I used to run before jobs because it cleared my head. And theater rehearsals—I used to actually show up for those, even when I was tired.\n\nNow it’s like… all of that feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can see the person who cared about it, but it doesn’t feel like me. If I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just… don’t care much. Even work, I just get through it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned thinking that dying would be a fair punishment for what you've done to your family and crew, can you tell me more about what you feel guilty about?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s a lot of little things that feel like they all point back to me. Like my husband got worse, and I keep thinking if I’d been different—less heavy, less… whatever I am—maybe he wouldn’t have. And with the crew, when we lost work, I kept thinking, that’s because of me too. Like I brought something bad onto the job.\n\nEven clients’ houses… if a floor didn’t come out right, or something got messed up, I’d look at it and think, see, that’s what happens when I touch things. I poison everything. And then my brain says, well, if you ruin people’s homes and your family and the people depending on you, you deserve whatever happens.\n\nSorry, I know that sounds dramatic. I keep thinking about this one kitchen tile job where the grout color was off, and the homeowner kept staring at it like I’d ruined her whole life. It wasn’t even just the grout, it was like proof. Like everywhere I go, I leave damage.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is just gone. Like, I’ll carry one box of tile and it feels like my whole body turns into wet cement. And then I’ll try to answer a couple client messages, just normal stuff, and my head gets so tired I can’t even decide what to say back.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, because that used to be nothing. I used to haul materials and keep track of measurements and colors and all that, and now I’ll stare at a message like it’s written in another language. Then I start thinking about how grout colors all have these names that don’t match what they look like—like “warm linen” or whatever—and I’ll lose ten minutes just stuck there, not even answering the person. So… yeah, slowed down, I guess. Tired in my body and my brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two weeks where you felt slightly better, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I can’t really point to a few hours and say, “yes, that was better.” Sometimes something distracts me for a minute, like if I’m looking at a tile pattern or… there was this one backsplash online with these little green pieces, and I kept thinking it looked like old bathroom glass from my aunt’s house, which is stupid because I haven’t been there in years.\n\nBut better? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. It’s more like I lose track for a little bit, not that I feel good.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm, beyond just not waking up?\n\nPatient: I mean… not like I can separate it cleanly. The not waking up thought is there a lot, and then sometimes it turns into, like, maybe dying would be what I deserve. Like a punishment that would balance out what I’ve done to my family and the crew.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as self-harm thoughts exactly… it’s more that my brain keeps saying I shouldn’t be here, or that things would be better if I wasn’t. And then I start thinking about work stuff again, like people depending on me and me ruining it, and it just loops.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.\n\nAnd theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. So… no, I don’t really enjoy things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… restless, I guess. Not like normal nervous energy, though. At work I’ll catch myself walking the same hallway over and over with my trowel still in my hand, like I’m supposed to be doing something but I can’t land on what.

And then I’ll check the grout lines again and again, even when I already checked them, and… I don’t know, there was this doorway the other day where I just stopped. Just froze there, because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. I know that sounds strange. It wasn’t like I was choosing to stand there. I know that’s not really answering with a number… it just keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like your work or spending time with your family?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I used to have things. I’d paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals, like too much food for everybody, and I used to run before jobs because it cleared my head. And theater rehearsals—I used to actually show up for those, even when I was tired.\n\nNow it’s like… all of that feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can see the person who cared about it, but it doesn’t feel like me. If I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just… don’t care much. Even work, I just get through it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned thinking that dying would be a fair punishment for what you've done to your family and crew, can you tell me more about what you feel guilty about?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s a lot of little things that feel like they all point back to me. Like my husband got worse, and I keep thinking if I’d been different—less heavy, less… whatever I am—maybe he wouldn’t have. And with the crew, when we lost work, I kept thinking, that’s because of me too. Like I brought something bad onto the job.\n\nEven clients’ houses… if a floor didn’t come out right, or something got messed up, I’d look at it and think, see, that’s what happens when I touch things. I poison everything. And then my brain says, well, if you ruin people’s homes and your family and the people depending on you, you deserve whatever happens.\n\nSorry, I know that sounds dramatic. I keep thinking about this one kitchen tile job where the grout color was off, and the homeowner kept staring at it like I’d ruined her whole life. It wasn’t even just the grout, it was like proof. Like everywhere I go, I leave damage.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is just gone. Like, I’ll carry one box of tile and it feels like my whole body turns into wet cement. I used to haul materials and keep track of measurements and colors and all that, and now I’ll stare at a message like it’s written in another language. Then I start thinking about how grout colors all have these names that don’t match what they look like—like “warm linen” or whatever—and I’ll lose ten minutes just stuck there, not even answering the person. So… yeah, slowed down, I guess. I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. So… no, I don’t really enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess. Not like normal nervous energy, though. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
More quickly? I don’t know if it’s quick exactly… it’s more like I can’t stop and then I stop too much, if that makes sense.

Like at work I’ve caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, back and forth, like I’m about to start something but I don’t. And then I’ll go check the grout lines again, and again, even though I already looked at them. My boss used to say you can tell a rushed job by the lines, and I keep hearing that, even though that was years ago and he was talking about a completely different job.

But then I’ll just freeze, too. I stood in a doorway the other day because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. So… not faster like I’m getting more done. More like my body is doing weird starts and stops.

Yeah… I mean, it happens more at work, but when I’m not working I still get that same feeling like I can’t settle.

That’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly how long… it feels like it’s been sitting there for a while, like a stain you keep seeing even when the light changes. I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn’t wake up, and then my mind goes off—like if I messed things up for my family or for my crew, maybe dying would be the fair punishment. I know that’s not really answering with a number… it just keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like your work or spending time with your family?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I used to have things. I’d paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals, like too much food for everybody, and I used to run before jobs because it cleared my head. And theater rehearsals—I used to actually show up for those, even when I was tired.\n\nNow it’s like… all of that feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can see the person who cared about it, but it doesn’t feel like me. If I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just… don’t care much. Even work, I just get through it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned thinking that dying would be a fair punishment for what you've done to your family and crew, can you tell me more about what you feel guilty about?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s a lot of little things that feel like they all point back to me. Like my husband got worse, and I keep thinking if I’d been different—less heavy, less… whatever I am—maybe he wouldn’t have. And with the crew, when we lost work, I kept thinking, that’s because of me too. Like I brought something bad onto the job.\n\nEven clients’ houses… if a floor didn’t come out right, or something got messed up, I’d look at it and think, see, that’s what happens when I touch things. I poison everything. And then my brain says, well, if you ruin people’s homes and your family and the people depending on you, you deserve whatever happens.\n\nSorry, I know that sounds dramatic. I keep thinking about this one kitchen tile job where the grout color was off, and the homeowner kept staring at it like I’d ruined her whole life. It wasn’t even just the grout, it was like proof. Like everywhere I go, I leave damage.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is just gone. Like, I’ll carry one box of tile and it feels like my whole body turns into wet cement. I used to haul materials and keep track of measurements and colors and all that, and now I’ll stare at a message like it’s written in another language. Then I start thinking about how grout colors all have these names that don’t match what they look like—like “warm linen” or whatever—and I’ll lose ten minutes just stuck there, not even answering the person. So… yeah, slowed down, I guess. Tired in my body and my brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two weeks where you felt slightly better, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I can’t really point to a few hours and say, “yes, that was better.” Sometimes something distracts me for a minute, like if I’m looking at a tile pattern or… there was this one backsplash online with these little green pieces, and I kept thinking it looked like old bathroom glass from my aunt’s house, which is stupid because I haven’t been there in years.\n\nBut better? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. Tired in my body and my brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two weeks where you felt slightly better, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I can’t really point to a few hours and say, “yes, that was better.” Sometimes something distracts me for a minute, like if I’m looking at a tile pattern or… there was this one backsplash online with these little green pieces, and I kept thinking it looked like old bathroom glass from my aunt’s house, which is stupid because I haven’t been there in years.\n\nBut better? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. It’s more like I lose track for a little bit, not that I feel good.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm, beyond just not waking up?\n\nPatient: I mean… not like I can separate it cleanly. The not waking up thought is there a lot, and then sometimes it turns into, like, maybe dying would be what I deserve. Like a punishment that would balance out what I’ve done to my family and the crew.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as self-harm thoughts exactly… it’s more that my brain keeps saying I shouldn’t be here, or that things would be better if I wasn’t. And then I start thinking about work stuff again, like people depending on me and me ruining it, and it just loops.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.\n\nAnd theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. So… no, I don’t really enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess. Not like normal nervous energy, though. At work I’ll catch myself walking the same hallway over and over with my trowel still in my hand, like I’m supposed to be doing something but I can’t land on what.\n\nAnd then I’ll check the grout lines again and again, even when I already checked them, and… I don’t know, there was this doorway the other day where I just stopped. Just froze there, because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. I know that sounds strange. It wasn’t like I was choosing to stand there. I just… couldn’t get myself to move for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving or doing things more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: More quickly? I don’t know if it’s quick exactly… it’s more like I can’t stop and then I stop too much, if that makes sense.\n\nLike at work I’ve caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, back and forth, like I’m about to start something but I don’t. And then I’ll go check the grout lines again, and again, even though I already looked at them. My boss used to say you can tell a rushed job by the lines, and I keep hearing that, even though that was years ago and he was talking about a completely different job.\n\nBut then I’ll just freeze, too. I stood in a doorway the other day because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. So… not faster like I’m getting more done. More like my body is doing weird starts and stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my work pants are loose now, like I keep having to pull them up when I’m carrying stuff.\n\nI don’t really eat right most days. I’ll forget until it’s late, and then when I do make something it’s like… three bites and my stomach turns. Which is annoying because I used to be the person who’d make a whole pan of something, you know? I had this one pair of pants that fit perfect for crawling around on floors, and now they kind of hang wrong. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or unable to sit still when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it happens more at work, but when I’m not working I still get that same feeling like I can’t settle.\n\nAt work it’s the worst, like I said—I’ll be pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, and then I’m checking the grout lines over and over like I missed something. My hands don’t know what to do. And then suddenly I’ll stop, like in a doorway, and I just… freeze there because it feels like if I move the wrong way something bad is going to happen.\n\nSo it’s not just fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I can’t make my body do the normal thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say completely unable, like tied to the bed or something… but there are days where getting up feels almost ridiculous, like my body is made out of wet cement.\n\nEven if I do get moving, by the time I carry one box of tile or answer a couple client messages, I’m just… done. My head gets so tired I can’t even decide simple things, like what to do next or which message to answer first. And then I’ll sit there staring at the phone, and it feels stupid because it’s just a message, you know? But it feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual, like your thoughts and movements are in slow motion?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, or maybe more like everything gets heavy and stuck. Like by the time I carry one box of tile, my body feels like wet cement. And even answering a couple client messages, which should be nothing, my head gets so tired I can’t pick what to do first.\n\nIt reminds me of when you’re mixing thinset and it’s too thick and the paddle just kind of bogs down—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. But that’s how it feels. Like I’m trying to move through something thick, and even simple decisions take forever.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

My head gets so tired I can’t even decide simple things, like what to do next or which message to answer first. And then I’ll sit there staring at the phone, and it feels stupid because it’s just a message, you know? But it feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual, like your thoughts and movements are in slow motion?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, or maybe more like everything gets heavy and stuck. Like by the time I carry one box of tile, my body feels like wet cement. And even answering a couple client messages, which should be nothing, my head gets so tired I can’t pick what to do first.\n\nIt reminds me of when you’re mixing thinset and it’s too thick and the paddle just kind of bogs down—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. But that’s how it feels. Like I’m trying to move through something thick, and even simple decisions take forever.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 40 / carpet_floor_or_tile_installer_or_finisher / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Loss_of_Interest\nmanifestation: \"I used to paint, cook big meals, run before jobs, and show up for theater rehearsals, but now those things feel like chores from somebody else's life and I don't care if I miss them.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really… I mean, I try to think of something small, but it’s hard.

I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals—like way too much food for one person, but it felt good, you know? And I used to run before jobs, which sounds awful now, but I actually liked it. I’d even show up for theater rehearsals… there was this one time we were building a set and I kept thinking the floor should’ve been tiled differently, which is stupid because it was just a stage floor—

But now all of that feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can see that I used to care about it, but I don’t feel the caring part anymore. If I miss it, I miss it. I don’t really… get much out of it.

I poison everything. And then my brain says, well, if you ruin people’s homes and your family and the people depending on you, you deserve whatever happens.\n\nSorry, I know that sounds dramatic. I keep thinking about this one kitchen tile job where the grout color was off, and the homeowner kept staring at it like I’d ruined her whole life. It wasn’t even just the grout, it was like proof. Like everywhere I go, I leave damage.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is just gone. Like, I’ll carry one box of tile and it feels like my whole body turns into wet cement. I used to haul materials and keep track of measurements and colors and all that, and now I’ll stare at a message like it’s written in another language. So… yeah, slowed down, I guess. Tired in my body and my brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two weeks where you felt slightly better, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I can’t really point to a few hours and say, “yes, that was better.” Sometimes something distracts me for a minute, like if I’m looking at a tile pattern or… there was this one backsplash online with these little green pieces, and I kept thinking it looked like old bathroom glass from my aunt’s house, which is stupid because I haven’t been there in years.\n\nBut better? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.\n\nAnd theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I stood in a doorway the other day because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. So… not faster like I’m getting more done. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. I know that’s not really answering with a number… it just keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like your work or spending time with your family?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I used to have things. I’d paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals, like too much food for everybody, and I used to run before jobs because it cleared my head. And theater rehearsals—I used to actually show up for those, even when I was tired.\n\nNow it’s like… all of that feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can see the person who cared about it, but it doesn’t feel like me. If I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just… don’t care much. Even work, I just get through it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned thinking that dying would be a fair punishment for what you've done to your family and crew, can you tell me more about what you feel guilty about?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s a lot of little things that feel like they all point back to me. Like my husband got worse, and I keep thinking if I’d been different—less heavy, less… whatever I am—maybe he wouldn’t have. And with the crew, when we lost work, I kept thinking, that’s because of me too. Like I brought something bad onto the job.\n\nEven clients’ houses… if a floor didn’t come out right, or something got messed up, I’d look at it and think, see, that’s what happens when I touch things. I poison everything. And then my brain says, well, if you ruin people’s homes and your family and the people depending on you, you deserve whatever happens.\n\nSorry, I know that sounds dramatic. I keep thinking about this one kitchen tile job where the grout color was off, and the homeowner kept staring at it like I’d ruined her whole life. It wasn’t even just the grout, it was like proof. Like everywhere I go, I leave damage.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is just gone. Like, I’ll carry one box of tile and it feels like my whole body turns into wet cement. And then I’ll try to answer a couple client messages, just normal stuff, and my head gets so tired I can’t even decide what to say back.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, because that used to be nothing. I used to haul materials and keep track of measurements and colors and all that, and now I’ll stare at a message like it’s written in another language. Then I start thinking about how grout colors all have these names that don’t match what they look like—like “warm linen” or whatever—and I’ll lose ten minutes just stuck there, not even answering the person. So… yeah, slowed down, I guess. Tired in my body and my brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two weeks where you felt slightly better, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I can’t really point to a few hours and say, “yes, that was better.” Sometimes something distracts me for a minute, like if I’m looking at a tile pattern or… there was this one backsplash online with these little green pieces, and I kept thinking it looked like old bathroom glass from my aunt’s house, which is stupid because I haven’t been there in years.\n\nBut better? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. It’s more like I lose track for a little bit, not that I feel good.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm, beyond just not waking up?\n\nPatient: I mean… not like I can separate it cleanly. The not waking up thought is there a lot, and then sometimes it turns into, like, maybe dying would be what I deserve. Like a punishment that would balance out what I’ve done to my family and the crew.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as self-harm thoughts exactly… it’s more that my brain keeps saying I shouldn’t be here, or that things would be better if I wasn’t. And then I start thinking about work stuff again, like people depending on me and me ruining it, and it just loops.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.\n\nAnd theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. So… no, I don’t really enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess. Not like normal nervous energy, though. At work I’ll catch myself walking the same hallway over and over with my trowel still in my hand, like I’m supposed to be doing something but I can’t land on what.\n\nAnd then I’ll check the grout lines again and again, even when I already checked them, and… I don’t know, there was this doorway the other day where I just stopped. Just froze there, because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. I know that sounds strange. It wasn’t like I was choosing to stand there. I just… couldn’t get myself to move for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving or doing things more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: More quickly? I don’t know if it’s quick exactly… it’s more like I can’t stop and then I stop too much, if that makes sense.\n\nLike at work I’ve caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, back and forth, like I’m about to start something but I don’t. And then I’ll go check the grout lines again, and again, even though I already looked at them. My boss used to say you can tell a rushed job by the lines, and I keep hearing that, even though that was years ago and he was talking about a completely different job.\n\nBut then I’ll just freeze, too. I stood in a doorway the other day because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. So… not faster like I’m getting more done. More like my body is doing weird starts and stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my work pants are loose now, like I keep having to pull them up when I’m carrying stuff.\n\nI don’t really eat right most days. I’ll forget until it’s late, and then when I do make something it’s like… three bites and my stomach turns. Which is annoying because I used to be the person who’d make a whole pan of something, you know? I had this one pair of pants that fit perfect for crawling around on floors, and now they kind of hang wrong. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or unable to sit still when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it happens more at work, but when I’m not working I still get that same feeling like I can’t settle.\n\nAt work it’s the worst, like I said—I’ll be pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, and then I’m checking the grout lines over and over like I missed something. My hands don’t know what to do. And then suddenly I’ll stop, like in a doorway, and I just… freeze there because it feels like if I move the wrong way something bad is going to happen.\n\nSo it’s not just fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I can’t make my body do the normal thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say completely unable, like tied to the bed or something… but there are days where getting up feels almost ridiculous, like my body is made out of wet cement.\n\nEven if I do get moving, by the time I carry one box of tile or answer a couple client messages, I’m just… done. My head gets so tired I can’t even decide simple things, like what to do next or which message to answer first. And then I’ll sit there staring at the phone, and it feels stupid because it’s just a message, you know? But it feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual, like your thoughts and movements are in slow motion?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, or maybe more like everything gets heavy and stuck. Like by the time I carry one box of tile, my body feels like wet cement. And even answering a couple client messages, which should be nothing, my head gets so tired I can’t pick what to do first.\n\nIt reminds me of when you’re mixing thinset and it’s too thick and the paddle just kind of bogs down—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. But that’s how it feels. Like I’m trying to move through something thick, and even simple decisions take forever.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I try to think of something small, but it’s hard.\n\nI used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals—like way too much food for one person, but it felt good, you know? I don’t really… get much out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would carry out the act of not waking up or considered a specific method to harm yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really have, like, a step-by-step thing. Yeah… I mean, I don’t really see things, not like that. But I do hear something sometimes.

It’s usually this man’s voice, behind me. Like at a job site, or even in my kitchen, which is stupid because the kitchen’s not that big, you’d know if someone was there. And it’ll say things like, “You missed it,” or “Everyone knows.” Clear, like somebody is standing right behind my shoulder.

At work I’ll turn around thinking maybe one of the guys came up behind me, because people do that when you’re kneeling and measuring, you know, they don’t think about how jumpy it makes you—sorry. But when I look, nobody’s there. It’s… it sounds real. That’s the part that scares me.

Like my husband got worse, and I keep thinking if I’d been different—less heavy, less… whatever I am—maybe he wouldn’t have. And with the crew, when we lost work, I kept thinking, that’s because of me too. Like I brought something bad onto the job.\n\nEven clients’ houses… if a floor didn’t come out right, or something got messed up, I’d look at it and think, see, that’s what happens when I touch things. I poison everything. And then my brain says, well, if you ruin people’s homes and your family and the people depending on you, you deserve whatever happens.\n\nSorry, I know that sounds dramatic. I keep thinking about this one kitchen tile job where the grout color was off, and the homeowner kept staring at it like I’d ruined her whole life. It wasn’t even just the grout, it was like proof. Like everywhere I go, I leave damage.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is just gone. Like, I’ll carry one box of tile and it feels like my whole body turns into wet cement. And then I’ll try to answer a couple client messages, just normal stuff, and my head gets so tired I can’t even decide what to say back.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, because that used to be nothing. I used to haul materials and keep track of measurements and colors and all that, and now I’ll stare at a message like it’s written in another language. Then I start thinking about how grout colors all have these names that don’t match what they look like—like “warm linen” or whatever—and I’ll lose ten minutes just stuck there, not even answering the person. So… yeah, slowed down, I guess. Tired in my body and my brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two weeks where you felt slightly better, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I can’t really point to a few hours and say, “yes, that was better.” Sometimes something distracts me for a minute, like if I’m looking at a tile pattern or… there was this one backsplash online with these little green pieces, and I kept thinking it looked like old bathroom glass from my aunt’s house, which is stupid because I haven’t been there in years.\n\nBut better? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. It’s more like I lose track for a little bit, not that I feel good.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm, beyond just not waking up?\n\nPatient: I mean… not like I can separate it cleanly. The not waking up thought is there a lot, and then sometimes it turns into, like, maybe dying would be what I deserve. Like a punishment that would balance out what I’ve done to my family and the crew.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as self-harm thoughts exactly… it’s more that my brain keeps saying I shouldn’t be here, or that things would be better if I wasn’t. And then I start thinking about work stuff again, like people depending on me and me ruining it, and it just loops.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.\n\nAnd theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. So… no, I don’t really enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess. Not like normal nervous energy, though. At work I’ll catch myself walking the same hallway over and over with my trowel still in my hand, like I’m supposed to be doing something but I can’t land on what.\n\nAnd then I’ll check the grout lines again and again, even when I already checked them, and… I don’t know, there was this doorway the other day where I just stopped. Just froze there, because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. I know that sounds strange. It wasn’t like I was choosing to stand there. I just… couldn’t get myself to move for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving or doing things more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: More quickly? I don’t know if it’s quick exactly… it’s more like I can’t stop and then I stop too much, if that makes sense.\n\nLike at work I’ve caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, back and forth, like I’m about to start something but I don’t. And then I’ll go check the grout lines again, and again, even though I already looked at them. My boss used to say you can tell a rushed job by the lines, and I keep hearing that, even though that was years ago and he was talking about a completely different job.\n\nBut then I’ll just freeze, too. I stood in a doorway the other day because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. So… not faster like I’m getting more done. More like my body is doing weird starts and stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my work pants are loose now, like I keep having to pull them up when I’m carrying stuff.\n\nI don’t really eat right most days. I’ll forget until it’s late, and then when I do make something it’s like… three bites and my stomach turns. Which is annoying because I used to be the person who’d make a whole pan of something, you know? I had this one pair of pants that fit perfect for crawling around on floors, and now they kind of hang wrong. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or unable to sit still when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it happens more at work, but when I’m not working I still get that same feeling like I can’t settle.\n\nAt work it’s the worst, like I said—I’ll be pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, and then I’m checking the grout lines over and over like I missed something. My hands don’t know what to do. And then suddenly I’ll stop, like in a doorway, and I just… freeze there because it feels like if I move the wrong way something bad is going to happen.\n\nSo it’s not just fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I can’t make my body do the normal thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say completely unable, like tied to the bed or something… but there are days where getting up feels almost ridiculous, like my body is made out of wet cement.\n\nEven if I do get moving, by the time I carry one box of tile or answer a couple client messages, I’m just… done. My head gets so tired I can’t even decide simple things, like what to do next or which message to answer first. And then I’ll sit there staring at the phone, and it feels stupid because it’s just a message, you know? But it feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual, like your thoughts and movements are in slow motion?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, or maybe more like everything gets heavy and stuck. Like by the time I carry one box of tile, my body feels like wet cement. And even answering a couple client messages, which should be nothing, my head gets so tired I can’t pick what to do first.\n\nIt reminds me of when you’re mixing thinset and it’s too thick and the paddle just kind of bogs down—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. But that’s how it feels. Like I’m trying to move through something thick, and even simple decisions take forever.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I try to think of something small, but it’s hard.\n\nI used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals—like way too much food for one person, but it felt good, you know? And I used to run before jobs, which sounds awful now, but I actually liked it. I’d even show up for theater rehearsals… there was this one time we were building a set and I kept thinking the floor should’ve been tiled differently, which is stupid because it was just a stage floor—\n\nBut now all of that feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can see that I used to care about it, but I don’t feel the caring part anymore. If I miss it, I miss it. I don’t really… get much out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would carry out the act of not waking up or considered a specific method to harm yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really have, like, a step-by-step thing. It’s more that I keep thinking I’d be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That’s kind of why I came in.\n\nAnd sometimes it gets tangled up with work stuff, like if I mess up a cut or someone has to redo something, I start thinking about how everybody’s carrying my weight—my family, my crew—and then it turns into this thought that maybe dying would be… I don’t know, like a fair punishment for what I’ve done to them. I know that sounds awful. I’m not trying to be dramatic. It just keeps showing up in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really see things, not like that. But I do hear something sometimes.\n\nIt’s usually this man’s voice, behind me. Like at a job site, or even in my kitchen, which is stupid because the kitchen’s not that big, you’d know if someone was there. And it’ll say things like, “You missed it,” or “Everyone knows.” Clear, like somebody is standing right behind my shoulder.\n\nAt work I’ll turn around thinking maybe one of the guys came up behind me, because people do that when you’re kneeling and measuring, you know, they don’t think about how jumpy it makes you—sorry. But when I look, nobody’s there. It’s… it sounds real. That’s the part that scares me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I’m not sure how to measure “worse,” exactly. The whole month’s kind of felt… heavy, like one long stretch instead of separate good and bad parts.

I used to haul materials and keep track of measurements and colors and all that, and now I’ll stare at a message like it’s written in another language. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.\n\nAnd theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. At work I’ll catch myself walking the same hallway over and over with my trowel still in my hand, like I’m supposed to be doing something but I can’t land on what.\n\nAnd then I’ll check the grout lines again and again, even when I already checked them, and… I don’t know, there was this doorway the other day where I just stopped. Just froze there, because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. I know that sounds strange. It wasn’t like I was choosing to stand there. I just… couldn’t get myself to move for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving or doing things more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: More quickly? I don’t know if it’s quick exactly… it’s more like I can’t stop and then I stop too much, if that makes sense.\n\nLike at work I’ve caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, back and forth, like I’m about to start something but I don’t. And then I’ll go check the grout lines again, and again, even though I already looked at them. I stood in a doorway the other day because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. So… not faster like I’m getting more done. More like my body is doing weird starts and stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my work pants are loose now, like I keep having to pull them up when I’m carrying stuff.\n\nI don’t really eat right most days. I’ll forget until it’s late, and then when I do make something it’s like… three bites and my stomach turns. I had this one pair of pants that fit perfect for crawling around on floors, and now they kind of hang wrong. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or unable to sit still when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it happens more at work, but when I’m not working I still get that same feeling like I can’t settle.\n\nAt work it’s the worst, like I said—I’ll be pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, and then I’m checking the grout lines over and over like I missed something. I can’t really point to a few hours and say, “yes, that was better.” Sometimes something distracts me for a minute, like if I’m looking at a tile pattern or… there was this one backsplash online with these little green pieces, and I kept thinking it looked like old bathroom glass from my aunt’s house, which is stupid because I haven’t been there in years.\n\nBut better? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. It’s more like I lose track for a little bit, not that I feel good.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm, beyond just not waking up?\n\nPatient: I mean… not like I can separate it cleanly. The not waking up thought is there a lot, and then sometimes it turns into, like, maybe dying would be what I deserve. Like a punishment that would balance out what I’ve done to my family and the crew.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as self-harm thoughts exactly… it’s more that my brain keeps saying I shouldn’t be here, or that things would be better if I wasn’t. And then I start thinking about work stuff again, like people depending on me and me ruining it, and it just loops.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.\n\nAnd theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. So… no, I don’t really enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess. Not like normal nervous energy, though. At work I’ll catch myself walking the same hallway over and over with my trowel still in my hand, like I’m supposed to be doing something but I can’t land on what.\n\nAnd then I’ll check the grout lines again and again, even when I already checked them, and… I don’t know, there was this doorway the other day where I just stopped. Just froze there, because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. I know that sounds strange. It wasn’t like I was choosing to stand there. I just… couldn’t get myself to move for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving or doing things more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: More quickly? I don’t know if it’s quick exactly… it’s more like I can’t stop and then I stop too much, if that makes sense.\n\nLike at work I’ve caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, back and forth, like I’m about to start something but I don’t. And then I’ll go check the grout lines again, and again, even though I already looked at them. My boss used to say you can tell a rushed job by the lines, and I keep hearing that, even though that was years ago and he was talking about a completely different job.\n\nBut then I’ll just freeze, too. I stood in a doorway the other day because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. So… not faster like I’m getting more done. More like my body is doing weird starts and stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my work pants are loose now, like I keep having to pull them up when I’m carrying stuff.\n\nI don’t really eat right most days. I’ll forget until it’s late, and then when I do make something it’s like… three bites and my stomach turns. Which is annoying because I used to be the person who’d make a whole pan of something, you know? I had this one pair of pants that fit perfect for crawling around on floors, and now they kind of hang wrong. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or unable to sit still when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it happens more at work, but when I’m not working I still get that same feeling like I can’t settle.\n\nAt work it’s the worst, like I said—I’ll be pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, and then I’m checking the grout lines over and over like I missed something. My hands don’t know what to do. And then suddenly I’ll stop, like in a doorway, and I just… freeze there because it feels like if I move the wrong way something bad is going to happen.\n\nSo it’s not just fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I can’t make my body do the normal thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say completely unable, like tied to the bed or something… but there are days where getting up feels almost ridiculous, like my body is made out of wet cement.\n\nEven if I do get moving, by the time I carry one box of tile or answer a couple client messages, I’m just… done. My head gets so tired I can’t even decide simple things, like what to do next or which message to answer first. And then I’ll sit there staring at the phone, and it feels stupid because it’s just a message, you know? But it feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual, like your thoughts and movements are in slow motion?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, or maybe more like everything gets heavy and stuck. Like by the time I carry one box of tile, my body feels like wet cement. And even answering a couple client messages, which should be nothing, my head gets so tired I can’t pick what to do first.\n\nIt reminds me of when you’re mixing thinset and it’s too thick and the paddle just kind of bogs down—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. But that’s how it feels. Like I’m trying to move through something thick, and even simple decisions take forever.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I try to think of something small, but it’s hard.\n\nI used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals—like way too much food for one person, but it felt good, you know? And I used to run before jobs, which sounds awful now, but I actually liked it. I’d even show up for theater rehearsals… there was this one time we were building a set and I kept thinking the floor should’ve been tiled differently, which is stupid because it was just a stage floor—\n\nBut now all of that feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can see that I used to care about it, but I don’t feel the caring part anymore. If I miss it, I miss it. I don’t really… get much out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would carry out the act of not waking up or considered a specific method to harm yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really have, like, a step-by-step thing. It’s more that I keep thinking I’d be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That’s kind of why I came in.\n\nAnd sometimes it gets tangled up with work stuff, like if I mess up a cut or someone has to redo something, I start thinking about how everybody’s carrying my weight—my family, my crew—and then it turns into this thought that maybe dying would be… I don’t know, like a fair punishment for what I’ve done to them. I know that sounds awful. I’m not trying to be dramatic. It just keeps showing up in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really see things, not like that. But I do hear something sometimes.\n\nIt’s usually this man’s voice, behind me. Like at a job site, or even in my kitchen, which is stupid because the kitchen’s not that big, you’d know if someone was there. And then I’ll try to answer a couple client messages, just normal stuff, and my head gets so tired I can’t even decide what to say back.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, because that used to be nothing. I used to haul materials and keep track of measurements and colors and all that, and now I’ll stare at a message like it’s written in another language. Then I start thinking about how grout colors all have these names that don’t match what they look like—like “warm linen” or whatever—and I’ll lose ten minutes just stuck there, not even answering the person. So… yeah, slowed down, I guess. Tired in my body and my brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two weeks where you felt slightly better, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I can’t really point to a few hours and say, “yes, that was better.” Sometimes something distracts me for a minute, like if I’m looking at a tile pattern or… there was this one backsplash online with these little green pieces, and I kept thinking it looked like old bathroom glass from my aunt’s house, which is stupid because I haven’t been there in years.\n\nBut better? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed that. It’s more like I lose track for a little bit, not that I feel good.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm, beyond just not waking up?\n\nPatient: I mean… not like I can separate it cleanly. The not waking up thought is there a lot, and then sometimes it turns into, like, maybe dying would be what I deserve. Like a punishment that would balance out what I’ve done to my family and the crew.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as self-harm thoughts exactly… it’s more that my brain keeps saying I shouldn’t be here, or that things would be better if I wasn’t. And then I start thinking about work stuff again, like people depending on me and me ruining it, and it just loops.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I used to have things. I used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals like I was feeding a whole crew even when it was just us, and I’d run before jobs if I could get up early enough.\n\nAnd theater too… rehearsals. I used to show up for that, even when I was tired from work, because it felt good to be around people doing something that wasn’t tile or carpet or invoices. But now all of that feels like… chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can picture the person who cared about it, but I don’t feel like her.\n\nEven if I miss rehearsal or don’t cook or don’t paint, I just don’t care. I keep thinking about how paintbrushes get ruined if you don’t wash them right away, and I have this one brush somewhere probably stiff as a stick now, but I still don’t get up to fix it. So… no, I don’t really enjoy things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess. Not like normal nervous energy, though. At work I’ll catch myself walking the same hallway over and over with my trowel still in my hand, like I’m supposed to be doing something but I can’t land on what.\n\nAnd then I’ll check the grout lines again and again, even when I already checked them, and… I don’t know, there was this doorway the other day where I just stopped. Just froze there, because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. I know that sounds strange. It wasn’t like I was choosing to stand there. I just… couldn’t get myself to move for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving or doing things more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: More quickly? I don’t know if it’s quick exactly… it’s more like I can’t stop and then I stop too much, if that makes sense.\n\nLike at work I’ve caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, back and forth, like I’m about to start something but I don’t. And then I’ll go check the grout lines again, and again, even though I already looked at them. My boss used to say you can tell a rushed job by the lines, and I keep hearing that, even though that was years ago and he was talking about a completely different job.\n\nBut then I’ll just freeze, too. I stood in a doorway the other day because it felt like if I moved wrong, something bad would happen. So… not faster like I’m getting more done. More like my body is doing weird starts and stops.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my work pants are loose now, like I keep having to pull them up when I’m carrying stuff.\n\nI don’t really eat right most days. I’ll forget until it’s late, and then when I do make something it’s like… three bites and my stomach turns. Which is annoying because I used to be the person who’d make a whole pan of something, you know? I had this one pair of pants that fit perfect for crawling around on floors, and now they kind of hang wrong. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or unable to sit still when you're not working?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it happens more at work, but when I’m not working I still get that same feeling like I can’t settle.\n\nAt work it’s the worst, like I said—I’ll be pacing the same hallway with my trowel still in my hand, and then I’m checking the grout lines over and over like I missed something. My hands don’t know what to do. And then suddenly I’ll stop, like in a doorway, and I just… freeze there because it feels like if I move the wrong way something bad is going to happen.\n\nSo it’s not just fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I can’t make my body do the normal thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say completely unable, like tied to the bed or something… but there are days where getting up feels almost ridiculous, like my body is made out of wet cement.\n\nEven if I do get moving, by the time I carry one box of tile or answer a couple client messages, I’m just… done. My head gets so tired I can’t even decide simple things, like what to do next or which message to answer first. And then I’ll sit there staring at the phone, and it feels stupid because it’s just a message, you know? But it feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual, like your thoughts and movements are in slow motion?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, or maybe more like everything gets heavy and stuck. Like by the time I carry one box of tile, my body feels like wet cement. And even answering a couple client messages, which should be nothing, my head gets so tired I can’t pick what to do first.\n\nIt reminds me of when you’re mixing thinset and it’s too thick and the paddle just kind of bogs down—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. But that’s how it feels. Like I’m trying to move through something thick, and even simple decisions take forever.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I try to think of something small, but it’s hard.\n\nI used to paint sometimes, and I’d cook these big meals—like way too much food for one person, but it felt good, you know? And I used to run before jobs, which sounds awful now, but I actually liked it. I’d even show up for theater rehearsals… there was this one time we were building a set and I kept thinking the floor should’ve been tiled differently, which is stupid because it was just a stage floor—\n\nBut now all of that feels like chores from somebody else’s life. Like I can see that I used to care about it, but I don’t feel the caring part anymore. If I miss it, I miss it. I don’t really… get much out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would carry out the act of not waking up or considered a specific method to harm yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really have, like, a step-by-step thing. It’s more that I keep thinking I’d be better off if I just didn’t wake up. That’s kind of why I came in.\n\nAnd sometimes it gets tangled up with work stuff, like if I mess up a cut or someone has to redo something, I start thinking about how everybody’s carrying my weight—my family, my crew—and then it turns into this thought that maybe dying would be… I don’t know, like a fair punishment for what I’ve done to them. I know that sounds awful. I’m not trying to be dramatic. It just keeps showing up in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really see things, not like that. But I do hear something sometimes.\n\nIt’s usually this man’s voice, behind me. Like at a job site, or even in my kitchen, which is stupid because the kitchen’s not that big, you’d know if someone was there. And it’ll say things like, “You missed it,” or “Everyone knows.” Clear, like somebody is standing right behind my shoulder.\n\nAt work I’ll turn around thinking maybe one of the guys came up behind me, because people do that when you’re kneeling and measuring, you know, they don’t think about how jumpy it makes you—sorry. But when I look, nobody’s there. It’s… it sounds real. That’s the part that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past month where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to measure “worse,” exactly. The whole month’s kind of felt… heavy, like one long stretch instead of separate good and bad parts.\n\nThere are days where I don’t get much done, but then I’ll think, well, some days at work even when everything’s normal you don’t get much done because the adhesive takes forever or somebody ordered the wrong trim—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed one specific period where it was clearly a lot worse than right now. It’s more like it’s been bad and stayed bad, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of acting on your thoughts of not waking up or self-harm in the past few days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of why I came in. The last few days it’s been in my head a lot, like, “it would be better if I just didn’t wake up.”\n\nI don’t know that I can say I’m… making a plan, exactly. It’s more like when I think about my family, or the guys at work having to deal with me messing things up, it turns into this thought that dying would be a fair punishment. Like I’ve earned it somehow. And then I get scared because I don’t want to keep thinking that way, but it keeps coming back.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
